| 430 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `arch` | **`meteredCall` accepts `userId: string \| undefined` and silently skips the charge when undefined — an unauthenticated route that should be Pro-gated would not get caught at the metering layer** (2026-05-07) | The contract is "anonymous routes pass undefined and skip billing" (see app.ts:492 visitor chat — intentional). Defense in depth: split the API into `meteredCall(...)` (REQUIRES `userId: string`) and `meteredCallAnonymous(...)` (explicit zero-charge variant). | 473 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `quality` | **`job_items.updatedAt` does not auto-bump on UPDATE — Drizzle's `defaultNow()` only fires on insert** (2026-05-09) | Identified while building the job-body-cleanse worker (#474). This is not guaranteed across job_items writers (scraper.ts, JobService, manual-post route, sentiment-score updates, etc.). First-pass cleanse over `IS NULL` works fine, so existing HTML gets fixed; re-cleanse on later edits is unreliable. Fixing unblocks: the cleanse worker noticing post-cleanse user edits, plus any other table that wants change-detection cursors. Options: (a) Drizzle `.$onUpdate(() => new Date())` on every `updatedAt` column, (b) Postgres trigger on UPDATE, (c) explicit `updatedAt: new Date()` audit at every write site. |

| 479 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `feature` | **Live-preview music doesn't resync on big seeks — playhead can be 10s into the timeline while music is still 2s in** (2026-05-09) | Initial implementation had a drift-detection effect that tore down + rebuilt the schedule when `currentTime` jumped > 0.5s, but it added churn (effect deps included `currentTime`, firing every RAF tick) and timing edge-cases caused premature rebuilds during normal playback. | 480 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `feature` | **Music lane width doesn't grow when a clip extends past `script.scenes` total duration — the clip is rendered but visually offscreen** (2026-05-09) | `NLETimeline`'s `totalDuration` is `script.scenes.reduce(...)`, audio extents are not considered. Side effects: a 30s render on a 12s video is truncated to 12s of audio at render time (preview + export both honor `endSec`); the user can drag-trim the right edge to extend later, but only up to the scene-derived total. Fix: change the timeline's working width to `max(scenesTotal, max-clip-endSec)` so a longer audio layer extends the rule + scrolls naturally; the playhead RAF should also cap at `scenesTotal` (video stops while music continues, then fades out). Identified 2026-05-09 after the user reported new clips not appearing on the lane (they were appended at `currentTimeSec + 30s` so extended past visible width). |

| 497 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `feature` | **No dispatch telemetry — when a call falls back across providers, we don't record which profile actually answered or how many attempts it took** (2026-05-09) | The `console.warn` lines in `tryProviderChain` and the dispatch loop in `llm-client.ts` are the only visibility into fallback events. Without this, we can't tell from production data which use cases are actually leaning on their cross-provider fallback (i.e. their primary model is unreliable in practice) — we just see a slow request. Fix: extend `tokenLedger.charge` with `{ profileChain: string[]; attemptCount: number; totalLatencyMs: number }` so SA can rank "use cases where the primary failed > 10% of the time this week". | 507 | 🔲 Open | Now | P2 | `feature` | **builderforce-sdk contract gap: gateway must NOT make model-selection decisions on our behalf — vendor / model / fallback choice is OUR business logic and stays on our side** (2026-05-09) | First framing of this gap had it backwards (asking the gateway to mirror our `USE_CASE_PROFILES` table). For that to work the SDK must accept and pass through verbatim: (a) explicit `model` per call (no "best available" substitution), (b) `temperature` / `max_tokens` / `top_p` (no clamping), (c) `response_format` typed (#500), (d) `tools` / `tool_choice` typed (#499), (e) any vendor-prefixed model id (`openrouter/...`, `cerebras/...`, `ollama/...`) routed to the correct upstream — we drive routing via the model string, not via gateway intelligence, (f) `useCase` accepted as opaque tenant slug for telemetry only (#501), (g) per-call `metadata` (#504) + `idempotencyKey` (#505) round-tripped on the response. Gateway responsibilities: HTTP transport, vendor authentication, per-tenant rate-limit, server-side observability (`_builderforce.resolvedModel` echo confirming our model string was honored without remapping). Unblocks: clean port that preserves our calibrated model choices + cost/latency/quality tradeoffs; gateway gets to focus on transport reliability rather than maintaining a 32-row product-decision table per tenant. |
| 508 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `feature` | **No gateway-side per-tenant cost reporting we can reconcile against our `ai_usage_records`** (2026-05-09) | Builderforce SDK exposes `client.usage.get({days})` but the shape (`UsageResponse.totals.totalTokens` etc.) is gateway-side aggregation, not row-level. Cross-checking gateway totals vs ledger totals for the same 30-day window is how we'd catch double-billing or charge-drop bugs. Fix: gateway exposes `GET /llm/v1/usage/records?since=...&cursor=...` returning per-call rows with `requestId`, `useCase`, `model`, `tokens`, `metadata` — pageable for reconciliation. Until landed: we trust the totals match by sampling `requestId` round-trips manually. |
| 511 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `feature` | **Gateway-internal retry billing semantics unspecified — risk of double-counting tokens against our user-side ledger** (2026-05-09) | The SDK response carries `_builderforce.retries: number` indicating how many times the gateway internally retried before returning a successful response. If the gateway charges the tenant key for each upstream attempt (typical pricing model), the `usage` we record from the final response captures all retries. We then debit that amount once on `tokenLedger.charge` — fine. BUT if the gateway returns 5xx after exhausting its internal retries AND we then advance to the NEXT profile in our cross-vendor chain (which the gateway doesn't know is a logically related call), the tenant has paid for both the failed retry burst AND the new profile's tokens. Our user-side ledger only sees the final successful charge. Result: tenant key spend > sum of user-side debits = gap. Fix: gateway documents whether `usage` on success includes retry tokens (yes/no), and whether failed-but-retried attempts are billed (yes/no). Builderforce SDK could expose `_builderforce.retryUsage[]` so our adapter can choose to debit each gateway retry separately. Until clarified: SA dashboard will under-report user-side spend vs tenant-key spend by the gateway-internal-retry delta. |
| 513 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `feature` | **Streaming + profile-chain fallback is architecturally incompatible — can't retry once we've started streaming bytes to the user** (2026-05-09, surfaced during contract audit) | None of our 31 call sites stream today, but the studio storyboard generator (#481 — currently 30s spinner) is a candidate. Problem: our `dispatchTextCompletion` walks `[primary, ...fallbacks]` and retries on failure. With streaming we'd commit to the primary's bytes immediately; we can't retry under the user without re-emitting "false" content first. Fix on our side (when streaming work begins): split streaming dispatch into "TTFB race" (try primary, on first-byte timeout fall over to fallback before either has emitted) vs "progressive" (commit to whichever profile produced first byte, no fallback). Tracked here so the architecture decision is recorded — out of scope for the port today, but unblocks any future "stream studio script as it generates" UX. |
| 515 | 🔲 Open | Now | P2 | `arch` | **builderforce gateway treats `model` as a HINT, not a hard pin — silent vendor substitution risks SLA breaches on latency-critical use cases (`match`)** (2026-05-09, surfaced during SDK 0.4.0 audit) | SDK 0.4.0 docs: "model is a hint... the gateway retains the right to substitute on cooldown, vendor outage, or plan-tier mismatch. (b) is cheapest and gives observability without locking out vendor improvements. (a) is the right long-term contract for latency-pinned use cases. Until decided: silent latency regressions on `match` go unobserved. |

| 536 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `quality` | **Email sync per-message LLM cost is unbounded — full-mailbox first sync on a heavy mailbox can fire hundreds of `email_classify` + `email_summary` calls in one cron tick** (2026-05-10) | [sync.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/email-integration/sync.ts) classifies every new message that wasn't caught by Stage-1 rules, then summarises every job-related message above 0.6. Heavy users (200+ unread inbox) on first connect could fire 100+ LLM calls in a single sync run — no per-user budget gate, no back-pressure, no batch dispatch. Today this is shielded by Stage-1 rules catching the obvious ATS senders, but if the user has lots of recruiter outreach below the rules' precision floor it can spike fast. Fix: (a) pass the user's `JwtPayload` into `runEmailSyncSweep` so per-user token-budget 429s land cleanly, (b) batch the summary stage (one LLM call summarising ≤10 messages at once via `callMessages`), (c) hard-cap per-sync-run classify count and roll the overflow into the next tick. Unblocks: predictable cost on full-mailbox-sync MVP. |
| 537 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `quality` | **Gmail sync fetches messages serially — bursts >50 messages will time out on a Cloudflare Workers sub-request budget** (2026-05-10) | [providers/gmail.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/email-integration/providers/gmail.ts) walks `users.messages.list` ids and calls `users.messages.get` one-at-a-time. ~300ms per call × 50 = 15s, ~200 messages = a minute, hits the 50-subrequest soft limit and the 30s CPU budget on Workers free tier. Outlook delta endpoint returns full message bodies in pages so it's fine. Fix: parallelise Gmail's `fetchMessage` loop with a small concurrency window (e.g. `Promise.allSettled` × 5), AND use `users.messages.batchGet` (not yet on the public REST API — would need the batch HTTP endpoint with a multipart body) when the count exceeds a threshold. Unblocks: reliable first-sync on heavy mailboxes. |
| 538 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `arch` | **No 429 / Retry-After handling on Gmail or Graph calls — provider rate-limit fails the whole sync run instead of backing off** (2026-05-10) | Both [providers/gmail.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/email-integration/providers/gmail.ts) and [providers/outlook.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/email-integration/providers/outlook.ts) throw on any non-2xx, which marks the `email_sync_runs` row `error` and the account `status=error`. Gmail returns 429 with `Retry-After` + per-user quota guidance; Graph returns 429 with `Retry-After` + per-app throttling. Fix: shared `providerFetch` wrapper that honours `Retry-After` (single retry, capped delay so a worker invocation doesn't exceed the 30s CPU budget) and downgrades 429 to a `partial` sync rather than an `error`. Unblocks: graceful degradation on transient throttling. |

Auto-created and dedup-linked emails should ideally show `"→ Senior Engineer at Acme"` so the candidate sees the connection without clicking through. Fix: extend `InboxExternalEmailDto` with `linkedJobTitle` + `linkedJobCompany` (joined in `inboxService` via `repos.jobs.findJobsByIds`), thread through `HubItem`, render below the preview line. Carry over to the row click handler so clicking opens the job page when a link exists. Unblocks: the candidate-side "see what email is about which job" close-the-loop UX that motivated this whole feature. |

Add a "json_schema retry rate by feature" widget to the existing AI use-case admin (`/api/superadmin/ai/use-cases`) so we can see which features benefit most from strict mode + which are still leaking budget on retries. | 549 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `arch` | **Frontend ships with no `VITE_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` — backend's `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` is set, so every login was being hard-rejected with `400 TURNSTILE_FAIL`** (2026-05-12, reported via `/login?source=extension` flow from the Chrome extension) | The site key is referenced by [TurnstileWidget.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/TurnstileWidget.tsx) and [use-turnstile.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/hooks/use-turnstile.ts) but does NOT appear in any `.env*` or `wrangler.toml` — Vite's build inlines it as `undefined`, the widget short-circuits via `if (!siteKey) return null`, and the form posts with no `cfTurnstileToken`. Backend was returning `TURNSTILE_FAIL` and locking everyone out (extension's "Continue in browser" tab + the in-popup email/password form both hit the same wall). **Mitigation in place (this commit):** [AuthService.handleLogin](hired.video/api/src/services/auth/AuthService.ts) now treats a captcha miss as a soft signal — records the bot_block_event and forces the email-OTP step-up after password verification (`captchaFailed → requiresMfa`). Real account holders prove humanness via inbox; bots without mailbox access stall at the second factor. Extension popup ([shared/login.js](hiredvideo-chrome-extension/shared/login.js) + [shared/login.html](hiredvideo-chrome-extension/shared/login.html)) hosts the OTP entry inline so users don't have to start over in a new tab. **Real fix still owed:** add `VITE_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` to the production frontend build (Cloudflare Pages env vars or `frontend/.env.production`) paired to the backend secret, so the silent challenge runs and only users who fail it get the OTP fallback. **Adjacent gap:** signup is still hard-blocked because [signupGuard.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/auth/signupGuard.ts) hasn't been given the same step-up rail (and adding email-OTP step-up to a NEW user creation flow is a bigger UX call). Unblocks: production sign-in for everyone, and re-enables silent bot defense once the site key ships. |

| 668 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `quality` | **`computeScriptHash` can't detect asset-URL content drift — if a scene references an R2 image whose contents change at the same URL the hash won't notice and a stale baked video would be served** (2026-05-16 — #665) | [baked-freshness.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/baked-freshness.ts) `computeScriptHash` hashes the asset URL string, not the asset's content. In practice scene assets are addressed by sha256-prefixed R2 keys so a content change ⇒ URL change; this gap only bites if a future writer mutates an existing R2 object in place. Fix options: (a) include asset Last-Modified / ETag in the hash via a small lookup, (b) enforce immutable R2 keys for all scene-referenced assets (current convention, just not load-bearing). Low likelihood, but the player will silently serve a wrong-looking video when it hits, so document the invariant either way. |

| 697 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `arch` | **SQL migration runner has no `_migrations` ledger — every deploy re-runs all 85+ files** (2026-05-16 — surfaced while fixing the failed 0085 deploy) | [api/scripts/run-sql-migrations.mjs](hired.video/api/scripts/run-sql-migrations.mjs) iterates every `drizzle/0*.sql` file on every deploy and only tolerates per-statement "already exists" errors. This forces every destructive op (DROP COLUMN, RENAME COLUMN, data backfills that reference dropped columns) to be hand-rolled idempotent — usually via `DO $$ ... END $$` guards on `information_schema.columns` / `pg_constraint`. 0085 shipped without those guards and broke the next deploy ("column video_id does not exist") because the runner also re-ran 0003 whose `CREATE INDEX ON ...(video_id)` now references a column 0085 dropped. Fix landed for 0003 + 0085, but the class of bug recurs on any future schema-rewrite migration unless the runner stores a `applied_at` ledger keyed by filename and short-circuits already-run files. Unblocks: schema rewrites stop being a deploy hazard; new migrations only need to be idempotent in their own right (not against earlier files re-running). |

| 708 | 🔲 Open | Now | P1 | `quality` | **Email-integration sync hits prod 500 — Drizzle UPDATE `external_email_accounts.last_history_id` fails (column missing) and the catch-path `last_error` write fails too, cascading into INTERNAL_ERROR** (2026-05-16) | Repro: ticket from sean@ on 2026-05-16T20:59 — `POST /api/integrations/email/sync/now` 500 with `Failed query: update ... last_synced_at=$1, last_history_id=$2 ...`. Root cause: production DB schema is behind code. Migration `0081_email_integration.sql` (delta cursors + rules + sync_runs) was not applied before deploy; `external_email_accounts.last_history_id` doesn't exist in PG so the cursor patch in [sync.ts:141](hired.video/api/src/services/email-integration/sync.ts#L141) throws, the catch tries to record the failure via `patch({ status, lastError })`, that ALSO fails (mechanism unclear — `last_error` IS in 0080 — possibly Neon-HTTP connection state or sub-request budget), and the unhandled rethrow becomes the 500. Mitigation shipped this pass: each diagnostic write in the catch is now wrapped in its own try/catch + console.error fallback so a follow-up DB failure can't cascade into a 500 (sync still returns its summary with `status: "error"`). **Update 2026-05-16T23:23** — second ticket from sean@ shows the same class of failure on a different surface: `POST /api/integrations/email/sync/now` 500 with `Failed query: insert into "email_sync_runs" ("id","user_id","account_id","started_at","completed_at","status","messages_synced","messages_classified","messages_excluded","messages_job_related",…)` for user 9f14dee7…, account 48496dfa…. Same underlying root cause (schema drift) but most likely a column from migration `0087_email_inbox_filters.sql` (`messages_excluded` / `messages_job_related` / `rules_fired`) missing in prod — the insert names them so PG rejects the whole statement. Surface: `emailSyncRuns.start()` was called BEFORE the try/catch block opened in [sync.ts:92](hired.video/api/src/services/email-integration/sync.ts#L92), so the failure bypassed the prior diagnostic-write mitigation and bubbled to the route as a 500. Additional code-level mitigation shipped this pass: moved the `emailSyncRuns.start()` insert inside the try/catch with `let run: { id: string } \| null = null`, and guarded the catch-path `run.patch()` with `if (run)` — so even when the audit row can't be created, the route returns a structured `{ status: "error" }` summary per account instead of a 500. Outstanding: (a) **apply migrations 0081 + 0087 to production** (`npm run db:push` from `hired.video/api`) — code-level robustness only masks the prod break; the feature stays broken until the schema is forward; (b) instrument the underlying Neon failure on the second write so we know whether to drop the `status` write entirely when the schema is mid-migration; (c) consider running a `SELECT column_name FROM information_schema.columns WHERE table_name IN ('external_email_accounts','email_sync_runs')` self-check at boot so a schema-drift deploy surfaces in logs immediately rather than on first user sync. Unblocks: Gmail/Outlook sync actually working in prod for connected users. |

| 714 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `arch` | **In-memory rate limiter buckets are per-worker — effective cap scales with worker count** (2026-05-16 — surfaced while fixing user-keyed rate limit) | [rateLimiter.ts:9](hired.video/api/src/middleware/rateLimiter.ts#L9) `store = new Map<string, RateLimitEntry>()` lives in a single worker's heap. | 742 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P1 | `bug` | **Rendered MP4 doesn't match the editor's Preview output** (2026-05-17 — reported by sean@: "the VIDEO that is being generated also doesnt match the version showin in the preview") | **Architectural finding on closer inspection (2026-05-17):** the per-frame canvas render path is ALREADY unified — both [VideoPreview.tsx::drawFrame](hired.video/frontend/components/VideoPreview.tsx) (RAF) and [video-exporter.ts::WebCodecs loop](hired.video/frontend/lib/renderer/video-exporter.ts) call the same `render(ctx, compiledVideo, time)` from [frame-renderer.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/renderer/frame-renderer.ts). The only divergence flag is `{ previewMode: true }` which suppresses the first-scene watermark in preview only. So the drift the user reported is NOT scene-graph divergence; it's in three async-asset / audio subsystems that the export doesn't synchronize with the preview's state: **(1) Font loading** — preview waits across RAF ticks for `document.fonts.ready`; the export's synchronous frame loop runs once and moves on, so glyphs render via fallback fonts if a webfont hasn't loaded by frame 0. **(2) Image assets** — `<img>` nodes inside scenes load async; preview eventually paints them as their `naturalWidth` materializes, the export captures a single frame per timestamp with no retry. **(3) Audio pipeline** — WebCodecs path muxes silence (no audio path at all); MediaRecorder fallback builds its OWN WebAudio graph in `exportMediaRecorderWithAudio` instead of consuming the shared [useMusicScheduler](hired.video/frontend/lib/audio/use-music-scheduler.ts) that #720 unified for preview. Concrete fix bundle: (a) `await document.fonts.ready` + walk every node in the compiled scenes to `await ImageDecoder.decode()` before the frame loop starts; (b) for the WebCodecs path, append an AudioTrack to the mux using a captured render of the shared music scheduler (offline AudioContext for determinism); (c) drop the bespoke MediaRecorder audio mixer and route through the same scheduler. Was originally framed as a renderer rewrite — the actual fix is much narrower. Unblocks: external MP4 publishes match the editor preview exactly. |

Adding a public endpoint only in (a) ships a 401 to production; the route file's intent is silently overridden. Fix options: (1) each route file exports its own `PUBLIC_PATHS: string[]` constant that app.ts unions, so the public list lives next to the handler. (2) Tag handlers via a `.public()` chain or `c.var.isPublic = true` set in a per-route middleware. (3) Replace the prefix allowlist with a per-handler `requireAuth` middleware applied at registration time — opt-in auth instead of opt-out. Unblocks: zero "forgot to allowlist" 401 regressions on every new public endpoint. The same pattern bit #324 / #747 in the past month. |

| 878 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P1 | `bug` | **JobEditForm ↔ UpdateJobSchema/`jobService.updateJob` silently drop ~15 fields — users edit Requirements/Responsibilities/EmploymentType/Salary/etc and the save returns 200 but persists nothing** (opened 2026-05-20 — surfaced building #876) | The form's `JobFormData` carries `requirements`, `responsibilities`, `atsApplyUrl`, `salary`, `bonus`, `equity`, `employmentType`, `workSetting`, `seniority`, `industry`, `hiringTarget`, `goLiveDate`, `expirationDate`, `status`, `privacy_ShowJob` and ships every one of them on `PUT /api/job/<id>`. [`CreateJobSchema`](hired.video/api/src/routes/jobs.ts) (which `UpdateJobSchema` derives from) accepts only `title`, `description`, `location`, `heroImageUrl`, `skills` — Zod's default strips unknown keys, so the route handler never sees the rest. Even if Zod accepted them, [`jobService.updateJob`](hired.video/api/src/services/job/JobService.ts) hand-rolls the SET clause off only those five fields, so the DB write would still drop them. Same class of bug that hit #871 (`patchMany` accepting fields by type but dropping them in SQL) and the 2026-05-04 `skills` fix referenced in the same schema comment. Fix is two-sided: (a) widen `UpdateJobSchema` to cover every editable column (probably mirror the JobItemEntity write surface — `requirements jsonb`, `responsibilities jsonb`, `employmentType int[]`, etc.), (b) widen `jobService.updateJob`'s field whitelist to match column-by-column, with the type-system enforcing parity (e.g. a `JOB_UPDATABLE_FIELDS` const derived from the schema and used as the source of truth on both sides). Test plan: a save-roundtrip integration test that flips every editable field, GETs back, asserts equality — the kind of test that would have caught both the 2026-05-04 `skills` drift AND this one. Unblocks: edit-page actually edits the job; pairs with #275 (Profile + Job page InteractiveCanvas integration) which assumes the JSON payload roundtrips. |

| 889 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `quality` | **Paid Cerebras call (`cerebras/llama3.1-8b`) returned 429 in 615ms via the Builderforce gateway — root cause unknown** (opened 2026-05-21 — surfaced building #888) | The 503 in `correlationId=llm-4c95a353` shows attempt 5 (`cerebras/llama3.1-8b`, `vendor: "cerebras"`) failing with `kind: "rate_limit", status: 429` after 615ms. That's much faster than a real Cerebras-side rate-limit response would surface (TTFT alone is usually ~200ms, plus queueing), suggesting the rejection happened gateway-local — either Builderforce's own per-tenant TPM/RPM cap on Cerebras is set lower than our usage requires, or the gateway's Cerebras account/billing tier is provisioned for a smaller burst than the chain assumes. Either is a config issue, not a code one, but it has to be diagnosed before the new paid-pool terminal in #888 can be relied on as a true safety net. Fix path: (a) reproduce by hitting `/api/admin/impersonate/ai/use-cases` + dispatch a Cerebras-only test from SA tooling, capture the full Builderforce response headers (rate-limit + retry-after), (b) if it's a Builderforce tenant cap, file the SDK gap and upgrade the plan or request a TPM bump; if it's a Cerebras billing tier under Builderforce, escalate to vendor onboarding. Cross-references #515 (Cerebras-only latency contract for `match`) — same vendor surface area. Unblocks: Cerebras stops being a flaky paid fallback; #888's pool can drop to Cerebras-only once headroom is confirmed. |

Fix: either re-seed the form state from `res.data` after each successful save, OR explicitly reject `""` with a user-friendly "slug can't be empty" toast and let the user re-enter one. | 915 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `bug` | **CommunicationHub popover doesn't surface the mailbox-reconnect banner — only the Outreach `/outreach?tab=inbox` tab and `/settings?tab=email` do** (opened 2026-05-21 — surfaced fixing the `invalid_grant` 502 toast on the Outreach Inbox) | [InboxTab.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/outreach/tabs/InboxTab.tsx) now mounts `<ReconnectMailboxBanner>` and [EmailInboxesPanel.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/email/EmailInboxesPanel.tsx) gets per-row `<ReconnectMailboxButton>` — but the header-popover surface ([CommunicationHub.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/CommunicationHub.tsx) → `<InboxPanel>` without the toolbar slot) renders the same merged feed with no reconnect affordance. A user who only ever opens the dropdown badge gets a stalled list with no obvious recovery path. | 916 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `bug` | **Mailbox reconnect happy-path assumes the user picks the SAME Google / Microsoft account at the consent screen — picking a different account silently creates a second row instead of healing the broken one** (opened 2026-05-21 — surfaced shipping the Reconnect flow for the `invalid_grant` toast) | [email-integration.ts /:provider/callback](hired.video/api/src/routes/email-integration.ts) upserts on `(userId, provider, accountEmail)`. The Reconnect flow at [ReconnectMailbox.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/email/ReconnectMailbox.tsx) reuses `emailIntegrationService.connectUrl(provider)` — which doesn't pass a `login_hint` to Google or Microsoft. If the user has multiple Google accounts logged in and picks a different one at the consent prompt, the callback's `mailboxEmail = await cfg.fetchAccountEmail(...)` returns the new address, the upsert misses the broken row's key, and a fresh `external_email_accounts` row is created. The broken row stays in `status: "error"`, the user's inbox now has two rows for the same logical mailbox, and the cap-counter (#107) treats it as two. Fix bundles: (a) thread the to-be-reconnected `accountEmail` through the state JWT in [emailIntegrationRoutes /:provider/connect](hired.video/api/src/routes/email-integration.ts) so the callback can detect mismatch, (b) set `login_hint=<expected email>` on the Google `authParams` + `login_hint=<expected email>` on Microsoft when reconnecting (not on first-connect), (c) on mismatch — bounce back to `/settings?tab=email&error=reconnect_account_mismatch` instead of upserting. Unblocks: a clean reconnect that doesn't leave orphan error rows in the table or quietly burn through the 3-mailbox cap. |
| 917 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `feature` | **No proactive token-health check — `invalid_grant` is only discovered when the user (or sync button) triggers an `/access-token` call** (opened 2026-05-21 — surfaced fixing the `invalid_grant` 502 toast) | The PWA-side sync at [sync-client.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/email-integration/sync-client.ts) only calls `/api/integrations/email/accounts/:id/access-token` when the user is on the Inbox tab and clicks Sync. A mailbox whose refresh token Google revoked overnight stays silently broken for days — the `status: "error"` flip in the route only fires on the next user-initiated sync. Worse, message backfill stalls without notification: users assume the lull is real-world inbox quiet, not a token-rotation failure. Fix: (a) a daily Worker cron `email-token-health` that calls `refreshAccessToken()` for every `status: "active"` row, marks the row `status: "error"` + `lastError` on `invalid_grant`, and skips the access-token result (we only need the refresh side-effect), (b) on first transition into `status: "error"`, enqueue a notification row ("Gmail (sean@…) needs reconnecting") so the user sees it in the same Inbox they're trying to use, (c) optionally email a transactional reminder if `error` persists > 72h. Unblocks: a user opens Outreach Monday morning to find a Reconnect prompt already waiting, instead of "why am I getting no emails?" Pairs with #915 (popover banner) — same surface lights up the notification. |
| 918 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `bug` | **`MAILBOX_REAUTH_REQUIRED` is detected via a regex match on Google's `invalid_grant` substring — Microsoft Graph returns at least three more codes (`AADSTS70000`, `interaction_required`, `consent_required`) that today's classifier mis-routes to the generic `REFRESH_FAILED` 502 toast** (opened 2026-05-21 — surfaced fixing the Gmail-specific case at [email-integration.ts /accounts/:id/access-token](hired.video/api/src/routes/email-integration.ts)) | [refreshAccessToken in oauth.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/email-integration/oauth.ts) throws an `Error` whose message is the raw provider response body. Today's route at [email-integration.ts](hired.video/api/src/routes/email-integration.ts) checks `/invalid_grant/i.test(message)` to flip to `MAILBOX_REAUTH_REQUIRED` — Google + Outlook both use that string most of the time, but Microsoft Graph also emits `AADSTS70000` (refresh token rejected — revoked/expired), `AADSTS50173` (recent password change, fresh sign-in required), `interaction_required`, and `consent_required`. All four are user-state ("re-consent to fix"), but today's regex routes them through the 502 toast path. Fix: parse the JSON response body in `refreshAccessToken`, return a structured `RefreshFailure { kind: "reauth" \| "transient" \| "config", message }` instead of throwing a string, and key the route's branch on `kind === "reauth"`. Centralises the provider-specific classifier in one place instead of leaking strings through the throw. Unblocks: Outlook users get the same Reconnect banner instead of a red 502 + Support Ticket prompt on every Microsoft re-auth needed. |
| 919 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `bug` | **No detection or migration path for `EXTERNAL_EMAIL_TOKEN_KEY` rotation — every existing refresh token becomes undecryptable garbage, the route then surfaces it as `invalid_grant` and floods every user with bogus Reconnect prompts** (opened 2026-05-21 — surfaced fixing `invalid_grant` toast) | [encryption.ts deriveKey](hired.video/api/src/services/email-integration/encryption.ts) derives an AES-GCM key from `EXTERNAL_EMAIL_TOKEN_KEY` (falls back to `JWT_SECRET` for dev). If that env rotates, `decryptToken()` either throws (caught) or decodes ciphertext to nonsense, the nonsense string gets POSTed as `refresh_token=...`, and Google returns `invalid_grant`. With #918's classifier in place, every mailbox would simultaneously transition to `status: "error"` and prompt users to reconnect — even though the tokens at Google's end are perfectly fine. Fix bundles: (a) version-tag the ciphertext blob (`v1:iv:ct`) so a key-rotation can ship `v1` + `v2` keys in parallel and decrypt either, (b) catch `decryptToken` failure specifically (vs. token-refresh failure) in [email-integration.ts /accounts/:id/access-token](hired.video/api/src/routes/email-integration.ts) and return `MAILBOX_DECRYPT_FAILED` (different code from `MAILBOX_REAUTH_REQUIRED`) so the UI can say "this isn't your fault — operator action needed" instead of asking the user to reconnect for nothing, (c) an admin migration script that decrypts-with-old-key + re-encrypts-with-new-key over the `external_email_accounts` table during rotation. Unblocks: safe key rotation + no user-facing mass-reconnect prompt when ops swaps the seed. |
| 920 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `quality` | **`public/sitemap.xml` hand-lists 14 audience routes + 20 template routes — drifts every time `AUDIENCES` or `TEMPLATE_CATALOG` changes** (opened 2026-05-21 — surfaced shipping #900 + #901 first cuts) | The sitemap entries for `/for/:audience` and `/templates/:slug` are static XML in [public/sitemap.xml](hired.video/frontend/public/sitemap.xml) but the source-of-truth registries — [audiences.ts](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/templates/audiences.ts) and [TEMPLATE_CATALOG](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/templates/catalog.tsx) — are TypeScript. Add a new template / audience and you must remember to hand-edit the sitemap, or its SEO ages out silently. Per the standing DRY rule the registries should be the single source. Fix: a Vite build-time plugin (or a one-shot `scripts/generate-sitemap.ts` invoked from `package.json` `prebuild`) that imports both registries, merges them with the existing hand-maintained legal / blog / tool entries, and writes the full XML to `public/sitemap.xml` on every build. Pairs with the hand-maintained `<lastmod>` field — the generator can stamp current date so freshness is honest. Unblocks: every new template / audience auto-indexes without a manual sitemap PR. |

| 933 | 🟡 Partial | Soon | P2 | `feature` | **Closed-loop asset interop — Phase 1 wired for image exports (the most common author→reuse loop); Phase 2 generalises to every kind + adds the `creator_assets` table for richer metadata** (opened 2026-05-22 — user reframe: "They can save the image and then import this into the studio for the game or video etc."; Phase 1 closed 2026-05-22 via the shared R2 `uploads/<userId>/images/` prefix + new `GET /api/upload/images` list endpoint + MediaPicker "Library" tab + DrawingStage "Save to library" button) | Today exports are downloads — the user gets an MP4 / WAV / GIF on their disk, then has to re-upload via `MediaPicker` to use it in another project. Design: (a) `RenderResult` gains an `assetRefId?: string` field — when set, the export already wrote the blob to R2 under `creator-assets/<userId>/<kind>/<projectSlug>.<ext>` AND created a row in a new `creator_assets` table (id, userId, kind, originProjectId, mime, sizeBytes, durationMs?, dimensions?, r2Key, createdAt); (b) the existing `MediaPicker` ([MediaPicker.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/shared/MediaPicker.tsx)) gains a "My Studio Exports" tab that reads from `creator_assets` filtered by the consuming project's mediaKind compatibility (video projects see videos + images + audio; game projects see images + audio + 3D models; CAD projects see images for textures; etc.); (c) per-mediaKind compatibility matrix in `@shared/media-kinds` — `canImportInto(sourceKind, targetKind) → boolean` so the Picker self-gates (image authored in `image` kind → importable into video / game / cad / interactive_comic / animated_film / etc.); (d) DRY: ONE export pipeline writes to R2 AND returns the local-download blob — caller can choose to surface the download UI, the "saved to library" toast, or both. Unblocks the "make it in one canvas, use it in another" loop the user described. Pairs with the existing voice marketplace (which already does asset-to-marketplace for voice clones via `studio_voice_clones`) — same pattern generalised. Compliance note: assets carry the originProjectId so deletion cascades correctly (delete the project → orphan its exports, prompt the user). |

(b) **Runtime interpreter** — `fireRules` + `executeActions` in [runtime-core.js](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/game/runtime-core.js). Triggers shipped: `on-level-mount` (one-shot per mount, gated by `hasFiredOnMount` flag), `on-tick` (every frame), `on-key-press` (matches transitions, not held), `on-click` (sprite hit-test in play mode), `every-seconds` (per-rule lastFired timer keyed by rule id). Actions shipped: `move-by`, `set-position`, `set-property` (width/height — mutates runtime instance only, never touches authored data), `show`/`hide` (toggles `visible`, drawFrame skips hidden sprites), `goto-level` (calls `mountLevel(targetId)` to swap level + reset sprites mid-tick — subsequent actions after goto-level are dropped because the scripted sprite was just unmounted), `set-message` (stamps a 2-second pill overlay above the sprite). (c) **Editor UI** — `<ScriptsEditor>` section in [GamePropertiesPanel.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/game/GamePropertiesPanel.tsx). Each rule is a card: trigger dropdown + per-trigger arg form (key picker for on-key-press, interval input for every-seconds) + a list of action rows + a "+ Add action" dropdown that creates the right action shape via `defaultAction(kind)`. Per-action arg forms render based on action.kind so the form always matches the data. (d) **Single-source guarantee** — the SAME `fireRules` + `executeActions` run in the editor's `<GameStage mode="play">` AND in the exported HTML5 bundle (the IIFE wraps `runtime-core.js` verbatim per the asset-cache pattern established in #960). No editor-only block runtime; no bundle-only block runtime. **Tests (25 new)** in [game-scripts-and-animation.test.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/__tests__/game-scripts-and-animation.test.ts) — pin every action's mutation contract + the goto-level drop-rest-of-rule guard + the on-key-press exact-match rule + the per-rule lastFired timer behavior. |

| 1007 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `feature` | **Real Snap!/Scratch DRAGGABLE VISUAL block editor — net-new gap because the flat-rule trigger+actions dropdown form currently mounted in [GamePropertiesPanel.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/game/GamePropertiesPanel.tsx) (per #973) does NOT satisfy the Snap-coding requirement the user named. The dropdown form is a baseline rule editor; the user explicitly wants the Scratch/Snap! visual programming surface non-coders recognise — a pannable block canvas, draggable nested blocks, snap-together control flow** (opened 2026-05-23 — user "Games is missing the SNAP coding"; #973 downgraded Done → Partial in the same pass) | The flat `BlockRule = { trigger, actions[] }` shape in [game-state.ts](hired.video/shared/game-state.ts) covers ~10% of what real visual programming demands. Missing primitives non-coders need: (1) **Nested control flow** — `if / else`, `repeat N`, `forever`, `repeat-until`, `wait-until` (the current `wait-seconds` is a single shape, not a control-flow block). (2) **Variables** — sprite-local and global, `set / change-by / show-monitor`. (3) **Expressions** — math operators (+, −, ×, ÷, mod, round, random), comparisons (=, <, >), boolean logic (and, or, not), string ops. (4) **Custom blocks / procedures** — user-defined reusable blocks with parameters (Snap!'s defining feature). (5) **Broadcasts** — `broadcast X / when I receive X` for sprite-to-sprite event wiring. (6) **Sensing** — `mouse-x / mouse-y / distance-to-sprite / touching-color / key-pressed (held, not just-pressed) / loudness / timer`. (7) **Looks** — costume switching (per-frame sprite swap), size scaling, ghost / whirl / pixelate effects. (8) **Sound** — volume / tempo / instruments. (9) **Lists** — array primitives (add / remove / get-item / length / contains). (10) **Pen** — drawing trail behind a sprite. To close: (a) Integrate `scratch-blocks` (MIT, the upstream Scratch core) or `blockly` (Google) — `scratch-blocks` is closer to the Snap! visual idiom. (b) Define a hired.video block vocabulary covering the 10 categories above. (c) Compile the block-tree DOWN to an EXTENDED `BlockRule` AST (the runtime stays the SoT). (d) Block editor mounts in the right rail when a sprite or the stage is selected — tabbed alongside the existing flat form (advanced authors keep the form; non-coders get blocks). (e) Runtime interpreter walks the new AST. Same single-source rule: editor + exported HTML5 bundle execute identical code. Reference: Scratch 3.0 / Snap! BYOB build a similar surface in <30k LOC. The flat-form #973 closure stays as a Partial — the dropdown form IS the AST's textual fallback. Pairs with #1006 (most useful blocks act on overlay widgets — `show overlay`, `hide overlay`, `set overlay text`). |

| 1024 | 🔲 Open | Later | P4 | `feature` | **Game physics — dynamic-vs-dynamic collisions + deep-penetration AABB resolution** (opened 2026-05-23 alongside the #1009 closure) | The hand-rolled AABB solver in [runtime-core.js](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/game/runtime-core.js) `stepPhysics` handles dynamic-vs-(static|kinematic) collisions cleanly but skips the dynamic-vs-dynamic case (`if (pb.kind === "dynamic") continue;`) — two boxes stacked on top of each other will pass through. Likewise the resolution uses the shallowest-penetration axis + body-centre comparison to pick the push direction, which breaks down when a body has passed the wall's centre in a single step (the resolver pushes the wrong way). To close: (a) Add dynamic-vs-dynamic with momentum exchange weighted by `mass`. (b) Use the body's velocity SIGN (not the centre comparison) to pick the resolution direction so deep-penetration cases push back along the entry path. (c) Optional: substep the integration when velocity per frame > body width (continuous collision detection). Reference: Box2D's discrete + bullet-body distinction. Until then the test suite documents the known shortcomings (the "deep penetration" comment in [game-physics.test.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/__tests__/game-physics.test.ts)). |
| 1025 | 🔲 Open | Later | P4 | `feature` | **`renderAnimation` runs synchronously on the main thread — large strips block the UI for hundreds of ms** (opened 2026-05-23 alongside the #1004 partial closure) | The GIF89a encoder + the rasteriser in [animation-export.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/animation/animation-export.ts) both run inline on the main thread. For a 480² strip with 16 frames the LZW pass alone takes ~50ms per frame on a modern desktop = ~800ms blocked time. Acceptable for the MVP because animation strips are typically short, but Web-Worker offloading is the proper home for the encoder so the editor stays responsive during long renders. To close: move `encodeGif` + the rasteriser behind a `Worker` (`?worker` Vite import) with `postMessage` chunking of frame buffers. |

To close: either (a) integrate SignalR / WebSocket push for `badge.unlocked` events (the SignalR client already lives at [signalr-service.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/services/signalr-service.ts) — the GamificationService's `onBadgeUnlock` would emit), or (b) have award-firing routes (POST /task/save, POST /feed/posts, etc.) return a `badgesUnlocked: PointsBadge[]` field in their response so the calling component invalidates `["points", "me"]` immediately. Option (b) is cheaper but only catches client-initiated unlocks; option (a) also catches server-side unlocks (e.g. nightly fraud sweep reversals). |

| 1161 | 🔲 Open | Later | P3 | `quality` | **Geo — `geocoder_cache` has no TTL/refresh; rows older than 90 days should be re-queried so renamed places + new postcodes propagate** (opened 2026-05-24 alongside #1154) | Mapbox results for major places don't drift over months, but new postcodes get assigned and rare renames happen (city merges, country code changes). | 1163 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `bug` | **Migration 0099_gamification_repeatable_badges.sql throws "duplicate key value violates unique constraint badges_slug_key" on each redeploy** (surfaced 2026-05-24 by the deploy of #1154) | The migration `UPDATE`s `badges.slug` from `first_task`→`get_it_done` and `streak_7`→`on_fire`. CI deploy log: `ERR ... stmt: UPDATE "badges" SET slug='get_it_done' … WHERE slug='first_task'` (and same for `on_fire`). The migration runner continues past the error so prod ends up partially migrated. To close: gate the UPDATEs on absence of the target — `UPDATE "badges" SET slug='get_it_done' WHERE slug='first_task' AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM "badges" WHERE slug='get_it_done')` (and same for `on_fire`/`streak_7`). Pairs with [[project_gamification]] #1134. Not part of #1154's geo scope — logged here per the "deferred issues" rule because the deploy surfaced it. |

| 1192 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `quality` | **`/api/auth/refresh` copies `emailVerified` + `mustChangePassword` claims from the existing JWT instead of re-reading from the DB — any state that flipped server-side stays stale on refresh** (opened 2026-05-25 alongside #1191) | [routes/auth.ts](hired.video/api/src/routes/auth.ts) `/refresh` handler reads claims out of the verified JWT and signs a new token with the same values. That's by design for `sub` / `email` / `roles`, but `emailVerified` + `mustChangePassword` are mutable in the DB and the refresh path doesn't re-read them. The verify-email path now sidesteps this by issuing its own fresh JWT (#1191), but any other code path that flips one of these flags (admin override, password-rotation flow) and then refreshes will keep serving stale claims. To close: re-fetch `users.{emailVerified, mustChangePassword, role}` inside the refresh handler before signing. Adds one DB read per refresh; the cost is amortised across the JWT's 7-day lifetime. |
| 1193 | 🔲 Open | Later | P3 | `quality` | **`emailVerifiedMiddleware` trusts the JWT claim with no DB fallback — any future "flip server-side then expect immediate effect" flow has the same stale-claim hazard #1191 documented** (opened 2026-05-25 alongside #1191) | [emailVerifiedMiddleware](hired.video/api/src/middleware/emailVerified.ts) is two lines: read `c.var.user.emailVerified`, 403 if false. There's no "fall back to DB on miss" branch. The pragmatic upgrade: when the JWT claim says false, do a single indexed DB read on `users.email_verified` and treat true-in-DB as verified for the rest of the request (cache it on `c.var.user` so downstream code sees the updated value). Pairs with #1192 — both are "trust JWT for speed, but recover gracefully when it's stale". |

| 1220 | ⏳ In Progress | Soon | P1 | `bug` | **CI deploy pipeline silently masks `db:push` failures, so SQL migrations apply onto a partially-bootstrapped DB and FK constraints to Drizzle-only tables fail** (opened 2026-05-25 — surfaced when [migration 0117](hired.video/api/drizzle/0117_recruiter_agent_followups.sql) deploy errored with `relation "recruiter_agent_runs" does not exist` despite the migration's defensive `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`). [.github/workflows/ci.yml](hired.video/.github/workflows/ci.yml) runs `db:push` (Drizzle schema sync) THEN `run-sql-migrations.mjs`. When `db:push` fails (e.g. [migration 0046](hired.video/api/drizzle/0046_sourcing_marketplace.sql) hit PostgreSQL's 1600-column limit on `job_items` because the out-of-tree legacy bootstrap left it bloated — see #1203), Drizzle-only tables like `recruiter_agent_runs`/`live_screening_sessions` never get created. SQL migrations then fail downstream with cryptic FK errors that look like the migration's fault. **Partial fix shipped 2026-05-25:** (i) [0117 hardened](hired.video/api/drizzle/0117_recruiter_agent_followups.sql) with a guarded DO-block FK addition so it survives this scenario; (ii) the four obsolete sourcing columns 0046 was adding to `job_items` (`accepts_sourcing_bids` / `sourcing_fee_cents` / `sourcing_deadline` / `sourcing_terms`) were dead — 0052 immediately drops them in favour of [`job_sourcing_listings`](hired.video/api/src/services/recruiter-marketplace/SourcingListingService.ts), and zero consumers remained across api/src/, shared/, or frontend/ — so they've been removed from 0046, taking the column-bloat blocker out of the deploy path. **Second fix shipped 2026-05-25** (after 0121's `archived_at` add on `job_items` hit the same 1600-column wall on the next deploy): (1) `run-sql-migrations.mjs` now tracks applied migrations in a runner-owned `_sql_migrations` table — each file runs at most once per database, eliminating the previous "replay every file every deploy" loop that made the `IF NOT EXISTS` defensive layer load-bearing; (2) [migration 0122](hired.video/api/drizzle/0122_reclaim_job_items_columns.sql) runs `VACUUM FULL "job_items"` to reclaim the dropped column slots — runs exactly once because the new runner records it as applied on success; (3) the failing `job_items.archived_at` add was extracted from 0121 into [migration 0123](hired.video/api/drizzle/0123_job_items_archived_at.sql) so it lands AFTER the slot reclamation; (4) [0121](hired.video/api/drizzle/0121_taxonomies_archive_versions.sql) had the broken `ALTER TABLE "job_items" ADD COLUMN "archived_at"` block excised with a header note pointing to 0122/0123. **Still open:** (a) make `db:push` failures fatal in CI by adding `set -e` and explicit exit-code propagation around the drizzle call (currently only the SQL-migrations step has `set -e`), (c) audit every Drizzle-only table in [KNOWN_MISSING_CREATE_TABLE](hired.video/api/scripts/migration-schema-parity.test.mjs) for downstream FK consumers and prepend defensive CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS in those consumer migrations the same way 0117 now does. Unblocks deploys against fresh / partial DBs without manual SQL intervention. |
| 1203 | 🔲 Open | Later | P2 | `test` | **Schema-vs-migration parity drift — 107 `pgTable(...)` declarations have no `CREATE TABLE` migration in `api/drizzle/`, and 9 migration-created tables have no Drizzle `pgTable` declaration** (opened 2026-05-25 alongside the #1186 fix + the new [scripts/migration-schema-parity.test.mjs](hired.video/api/scripts/migration-schema-parity.test.mjs) regression suite) — root cause of the #1186 deploy failure was systemic: the core schema (`users`, `resumes`, `jobs`, `studio_projects`, etc.) was originally bootstrapped via an out-of-tree `drizzle-kit push` and only migrations from 0001 onward live in version control. 107 tables are frozen in the test's `KNOWN_MISSING_CREATE_TABLE` baseline (the ratchet prevents *new* drift; existing entries shrink as backfill migrations land). The 9 orphan tables (`promo_project_reviews`, `company_reviews`, `sourced_people`, `sourced_person_merge_log`, `person_identifiers`, `person_sources`, `enrichment_cache`, `data_subject_requests`, `data_suppression_list`) are migration leftovers from polymorphic refactors and the BurnRateOS sourcing-pipeline split (0054 — see project_contact_sourcing_burnrateos.md) where the table was created but never declared in Drizzle, or declared and later removed without a DROP migration. To close: (a) write a backfill `0Bxx_legacy_schema_baseline.sql` capturing the current canonical CREATE TABLE for every entry in `KNOWN_MISSING_CREATE_TABLE` (use `pg_dump --schema-only` against production as the source of truth), (b) ship targeted DROP migrations for the orphan tables that genuinely went away, (c) shrink the baseline list and the `ALLOWED_ORPHANS` set in the parity test as each one is reconciled. Unblocks fresh-DB bring-up of dev environments without manual SQL + makes the test fail loud the moment ANY drift returns. |

| 1232 | 🔲 Open | Later | P3 | `feature` | **Predictive Assessments battery ships validated-INSPIRED instruments — not psychometrically normed scores** (opened 2026-05-25 alongside the [predictive-assessments tool](hired.video/frontend/pages/tools/PredictiveAssessments.tsx) launch). The Big Five uses the public-domain IPIP-NEO short form (10 items); the cognitive sample, SJT, EQ, and DISC are platform-derived heuristic batteries that score the right things but aren't calibrated against a representative norming sample. HireVue's 20+-year moat is exactly this calibration — scores you can defend in court for adverse-impact analysis. To close: (a) collect anonymous response data from the existing `assessment_runs` table, (b) compute platform-level percentile distributions per dimension per assessment, (c) update the scoring service to convert raw 0..1 scores into z-scores against the platform norms, (d) publish a methodology note + adverse-impact analysis so employers can defensibly use scores in screening. Until then, the on-page copy is explicit that scores are descriptive, not predictive — and the assessments are not surfaced to recruiters as a screening filter. Unblocks: positioning the assessment battery as a screening signal rather than a candidate self-discovery tool, full HireVue parity claim. |

| 1361 | ⚠️ Partial | Soon | P2 | `perf` | **Feature-flag DB cache — per-isolate only, no cross-isolate invalidation** ([featureflag.ts](hired.video/api/src/routes/featureflag.ts)) (opened + half-FIXED 2026-05-25). **Closed**: `GET /api/featureflag` and `GET /api/featureflag/:key` now share an in-isolate cache with 60 s TTL + admin-write invalidation via `invalidateFeatureFlagCache()`. Previously every read fired 2 SELECT queries against Neon (~10–20 ms each) for a mostly-static table — multiplied by every request that gates a UI feature. **Remaining**: cache is per-Worker-isolate, so admin flips propagate to other isolates only when their TTL expires (≤ 60 s staleness). | 1362 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `perf` | **Creator dashboard analytics — N+1 artifact metadata + per-user `getSubscription` fan-out** (opened 2026-05-25 — `services/analytics/creatorRollup.ts:67-99` + `services/creators/visibility.ts:45-62`). Dashboard load fires `Promise.all(items.map(async i => db.select(...).where(eq(table.x, i.x))))` — one Neon round-trip per artifact. For 200 artifacts that's 200+ round-trips when a single `inArray()` join would do it. Same anti-pattern on `getSubscription(userId)` fanned out across visible users. Est. impact: 300–500 ms on creator dashboard load; OOM-class on creators with thousands of artifacts. How to apply: rewrite the per-artifact unique-viewer count as a single `GROUP BY artifactId` query; replace the per-user `getSubscription(id)` fan-out with `inArray()` + a Map lookup. |
| 1363 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `perf` | **Gamification wrapper rebuilds all 31 repos per route event** ([domains/gamification/with-gamification.ts:37-72](hired.video/api/src/domains/gamification/with-gamification.ts)) (opened 2026-05-25). `withGamification(c, label, fn)` calls `gamificationFromContext(c)` → `buildRepos(getDb())` on every fire, constructing all 31 repository instances even though only `points` is needed. Routes that fire multiple gamification events per request (`feed.ts`, `jobs.ts`, `search.ts`, `tool-runs.ts`) pay this overhead repeatedly. Est. impact: +5–10 ms per gamification event on hot routes. How to apply: cache the repos singleton per request context (`c.set("repos", repos)` once in middleware, `c.get("repos")` everywhere else), OR lazy-init individual repos inside `GamificationService` constructor. |

| 1365 | 🔲 Open | Later | P4 | `perf` | **JWT secret read on every request from rateLimiter + auth middleware** ([rateLimiter.ts:105](hired.video/api/src/middleware/rateLimiter.ts), [middleware/auth.ts](hired.video/api/src/middleware/auth.ts)) (opened 2026-05-25). `getJwtSecret()` / `getJwtSecretOptional()` reads `process.env.JWT_SECRET` every call. Negligible per-call cost (1–2 µs) but trivially eliminable. How to apply: encode the secret once at module scope (`const SECRET_KEY = new TextEncoder().encode(getJwtSecret())`) and import the precomputed key. |

With the recruiter extension now invoking the picker in three modes against the same host (LinkedIn surfaces all three), each capture clobbers the previous mode's selectors instead of accumulating per-mode replay data. To close: switch the key to `${host}|${mode}` (or `all[host] = { job: {...}, profile: {...}, company: {...} }`) and update `clearLearned` accordingly. Low blast radius — replay is best-effort and the heuristic auto-suggest still works without saved selectors. |

Picker now learns the user's site once and replays on every subsequent visit — biggest win for the recruiter profile / company modes whose heuristic auto-suggest is weaker than `detectJobInPage`. Original notes: (opened 2026-05-25 alongside #946 ship). Telemetry stores captured selectors via `saveLearned()` after every picker run, but neither extension currently calls `loadLearned(host)` at the start of `autoSuggestFor` to skip manual second-time-around picking. The jobseeker hides this via `detectJobInPage` working well on common sites; recruiter profile / company modes lean on weaker heuristics and would benefit more. To close: at `enterStep()` boot, look up the saved selector for the current `(host, mode, key)` triple and use it as the first auto-suggest before falling back to heuristics. Pair with #1395 so the keying is mode-aware first. |

| 1448 | 🔲 Open | Later | P3 | `perf` | **Drizzle-side projection for DTO-assembled list routes (#1445 phase 2)** (opened 2026-05-25 alongside #1445 partial). Every other list route besides `/api/notifications/` goes through a DTO assembler (`rowToCompanyMeta`, `toPublicCard`, `fetchPostDtos`, `toPublicUserCardDto`, `rowToTalentPoolEntry`) that reads the full DB row to compose downstream fields. To push projection through to the SELECT, each DTO needs to either: (a) accept a `fields` parameter and conditionally skip the columns the caller didn't request, OR (b) be split into "must-have base columns" + "optional columns by name" so the route can compose the SELECT shape dynamically. ~3 hours per endpoint. Most DTO assemblers do trivial mapping (`row.name → dto.name`) so option (a) is mostly mechanical. Worth it once a real Neon I/O budget complaint surfaces — until then, the Worker→client wire trim (#1444) is the dominant saving. |

| 1472 | 🔲 Open | Later | P3 | `quality` | **`api/src/services/__tests__/ai-billing-coverage.test.ts` heuristic could miss truly-unmetered call sites when systemPrompt + userMessage exceed 3000 chars** (opened 2026-05-25 during the test runner fix). The window-based detection now uses 3000 chars (was 600) to accommodate long template literals in [dashboard.ts](hired.video/api/src/routes/dashboard.ts) etc. The trade-off: if someone ships a route with a 4000-char prompt AND forgets `useCase:`, the test won't flag it. The right fix is an AST-based walker that finds the `callJson` call expression and inspects its sibling argument object's properties for `useCase`. | 1473 | ✅ Done | Now | P1 | `quality` | **`baseUrl: "."` in [tsconfig.json](hired.video/tsconfig.json) + [api/tsconfig.json](hired.video/api/tsconfig.json) tripped TS5101 deprecation under TS 5.9** (resolved 2026-05-26). Removed `baseUrl` from both configs; the existing `paths` mappings (`@/*`, `@shared/*`) resolve correctly without it because TS 5+ resolves path mappings relative to the tsconfig.json that contains them. The `--traceResolution` log for the prior `three` lookup confirmed baseUrl was being tried but never produced a successful resolution — every match came from node_modules or the `paths` mapping. Vite (`vite.config.ts` resolve.alias) + ESLint (`eslint-import-resolver-typescript`) use independent resolvers and are unaffected. Caveat: if anyone later writes non-relative imports that aren't prefixed with `@` / `@shared` (e.g. `import x from "frontend/lib/foo"`), they'll silently fail to resolve — but no such imports exist today. Unblocks: clean migration path to TS 7.0. |

| 1513 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `bug` | **CI install retry loop swallows persistent failures — `for i in 1 2 3; do npm install … && break; sleep 5; done` exits 0 even when all 3 attempts fail** (opened 2026-05-26 after the typecheck-against-empty-node_modules outage that motivated #1507's Tiptap install). The loop's overall exit code is the last `sleep` (always 0), so a hard install failure leaves node_modules unpopulated and silently lets the next step run with "Cannot find module 'react'" everywhere. Fix: track success in a variable and `exit 1` after the loop if none of the attempts succeeded, or chain three invocations via `||` so the overall command propagates the last non-zero exit. Applies to every install step in [.github/workflows/ci.yml](hired.video/.github/workflows/ci.yml). The 2026-05-26 fix swapping `npm ci` → `npm install` removes the lockfile-drift trigger but does not fix the latent bug. |
| 1514 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `bug` | **`DeclarativeResumeRenderer`'s `projects` case ignores `metaData` — actor / director / film-TV templates never render production stills or reels per credit** (opened 2026-05-26). Data model + editor flow already support per-credit media: `ResumeSectionMeta` rows bind images / videos / YouTube to any section item via `referenceId = project.id` ([shared/resume.ts:358-369](hired.video/shared/resume.ts#L358-L369)), the resume editor's `MetadataModal` lists every project in its "Choose section" dropdown ([components/MetadataModal.tsx:207-209](hired.video/frontend/components/MetadataModal.tsx#L207-L209)) and even offers a **Studio picker** that pulls published Studio videos + thumbnails as attachable assets. The classic [ResumeViewer.tsx:722-730](hired.video/frontend/components/ResumeViewer.tsx#L722-L730) renders them via `MetaBadge`. But the new declarative renderer that powers every marketplace template ([DeclarativeResumeRenderer.tsx:910-945](hired.video/frontend/components/resume-renderer/DeclarativeResumeRenderer.tsx#L910-L945)) silently drops them — `case "projects":` only reads name / url / dates / entity / description / highlights / keywords / `ItemSkillBadges`. So an actor who attaches a still to "Hamlet · Laertes · Royal Court" sees nothing on the rendered resume or at [/marketplace/templates/actor-headshot-hero](https://hired.video/marketplace/templates/actor-headshot-hero). Fix: in the renderer, when the section descriptor opts in (see #1515), render `data.metaData?.filter(m => m.referenceId === p.id)` as a thumbnail strip (image tile + video poster) above the highlights, reusing `MetaBadge` for parity with the classic viewer. Unblocks: visual credit walls on every film / theatre / TV / music template, plus matches the JSON-Resume round-trip the data already produces. |
| 1515 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `feature` | **`projects` section descriptor has no `showMedia` flag — `actor-headshot-hero` + `director-filmography-serif` ship as text-only despite being media-led industries** (opened 2026-05-26, depends on #1514). [`ResumeSectionConfig`](hired.video/frontend/types/resume-template.ts) (the per-section bag inside `ResumeTemplateDescriptor.sections`) already carries `layout`, `showHighlights`, `sortBy`, `columns`, `icon`, `title` — but no opt-in for media surfacing. Without a flag the renderer can't tell which templates SHOULD lean visual (Actor — Headshot Hero, Director — Filmography Serif, future music / influencer / photographer templates) vs which should stay text-only (Software Engineer — Dev Graphite, Executive — Print Classic). Fix: add `showMedia?: boolean` to the section descriptor, default `false`, set `true` on [ACTOR_HEADSHOT_HERO line 404](hired.video/frontend/types/resume-template.ts#L404) and [DIRECTOR_FILMOGRAPHY_SERIF line 441](hired.video/frontend/types/resume-template.ts#L441) projects entries. Optional follow-up: a media-led `layout: "cards"` variant for projects that promotes the thumbnail to the card header (poster-first credit cards) rather than a strip beneath the text. Unblocks: per-credit production stills surfacing in the marketplace preview for film / theatre / TV templates without forcing every other template to show media. |

| 1516 | ✅ Done | — | P0 | `bug` | **Seeded SCORM-library lessons can't save progress — `md5(...)::uuid` produces non-RFC-9562 UUIDs that Zod 4 `.uuid()` rejects** (already fixed in master prior to 2026-05-26; the symptom Sean hit on prod was deploy lag). Resolution: every uuid validator on the courses + xAPI body paths uses [`UuidLikeSchema`](hired.video/shared/validation.ts#L41) (permissive 8-4-4-4-12 hex regex matching what PostgreSQL accepts) instead of Zod 4's strict `.uuid()`. Strict `.uuid()` is reserved for app-generated IDs. |

| 1522 | 🔲 Open | Later | P3 | `feature` | **TextNarrator could swap Web Speech for the existing WebGPU TTS engines + R2-cached audio blobs** (opened 2026-05-26 during #1519 close). Today's narrator uses `speechSynthesis.speak()` — zero infra, works everywhere, but the voice quality is browser-OS dependent (Edge's neural voices > Chrome's > Firefox's). The richer path: call the [WebGPU TTS engines](hired.video/frontend/lib/services/tts-engines.ts) (Kokoro-82M / Piper) that already power Studio voiceovers, cache the resulting MP3 per `(lessonId, voiceId, rate)` tuple on R2, and serve cached audio on repeat plays. Cost: zero per repeat play (CDN hit), one local GPU synth on first play, gracefully degrades to Web Speech on non-WebGPU browsers. Estimated ~3 days. Self-contained inside `<TextNarrator>` — no consumer changes. |

| 1524 | 🔲 Open | Later | P4 | `bug` | **`/api/courses/me/enrollments` returns 200 with `[]` on auth failure — frontend can't distinguish "you have no enrollments" from "your session expired"** (opened 2026-05-26 during the unenroll work). [CoursePublicDetail.tsx:165-179](hired.video/frontend/pages/marketplace/CoursePublicDetail.tsx#L165-L179) uses `courseService.listMyEnrollments()` to decide whether to show "Enroll" vs "Continue learning". When auth fails silently, the route returns `success([])` and the page renders "Enroll" → user clicks → POST `/api/courses/:id/enroll` 401s → toast says "Couldn't enroll". A more accurate failure mode is to either (a) require auth on the listing route + surface 401 to the caller, or (b) attach a `__authState` field to the response so the consumer can decide. Today the silent-empty pattern hides the auth signal everywhere it's used. Pairs with the broader auth-state plumbing audit. |
| 1522 | ✅ Done | Now | P1 | `bug` | **Seeded `md5(slug)::uuid` IDs fail Zod 4 `.uuid()` validation — every route that strict-validated a DB-row UUID rejected seed-derived rows** (opened + fully fixed 2026-05-26 after a support ticket: PATCH `/api/courses/enrollments/:id/progress` returned 400 `VALIDATION_ERROR` "Invalid UUID" with body `{ lessonId: "c87f7656-673a-7756-d100-281032a50731", … }` — that lesson is from [0131_seed_platform_learning_library.sql](hired.video/api/drizzle/0131_seed_platform_learning_library.sql) which uses `md5('seed-lesson:…')::uuid` for deterministic IDs). Root cause: Zod 4's `.uuid()` regex requires the variant nibble to be `[89abAB]` (RFC 4122), but MD5 hashes land on any nibble — the user's lesson ID has variant `d`. Same seed pattern is in [0113_so_you_want_to_become_articles.sql](hired.video/api/drizzle/0113_so_you_want_to_become_articles.sql) for seeded articles. **Fix**: introduced `UuidLikeSchema` + `UUID_LIKE_REGEX` in [shared/validation.ts](hired.video/shared/validation.ts) (permissive 8-4-4-4-12 hex, no version/variant constraint) and **swept the entire codebase** — 270 call sites across 57 files in `api/src/` + `shared/` migrated from `z.string().uuid()` → `UuidLikeSchema` (the two custom-message callers in [superadmin/sessions.ts](hired.video/api/src/routes/superadmin/sessions.ts) + [superadmin/users.ts](hired.video/api/src/routes/superadmin/users.ts) preserve their messages via `z.string().regex(UUID_LIKE_REGEX, "…")`). Strict `UuidSchema` is kept in shared/validation.ts for IDs generated fresh in app code; doc comment in the file pins the split. **Latent Zod-4 bug also fixed in the same pass**: two `z.record(z.unknown())` calls in [courses.ts](hired.video/api/src/routes/courses.ts) (ScormCommitSchema.cmi, XapiStatementSchema.context) were silently broken under Zod 4 (which requires `z.record(key, value)`); both now `z.record(z.string(), z.unknown())`. **Tests added** (3 new files, 72 cases total — closes the test-coverage gap that masked this bug): (a) [shared/__tests__/validation.test.ts](hired.video/shared/__tests__/validation.test.ts) — 25 cases pinning `UuidSchema` strict + `UuidLikeSchema` permissive + raw regex against the prod-incident UUID, runtime v4s, RFC v1/v7, nil/max, uppercase, shape-length drift, non-hex, whitespace, null/undefined/numbers; (b) [api/src/routes/__tests__/courses-schemas.test.ts](hired.video/api/src/routes/__tests__/courses-schemas.test.ts) — 38 cases against `ProgressUpdateSchema`, `ScormCommitSchema`, `XapiStatementSchema`, `PrerequisiteSchema`, `CreateLessonSchema` (happy + boundary + exception, with explicit md5-seed-derived UUID fixtures); (c) [api/src/routes/__tests__/learning-paths-schemas.test.ts](hired.video/api/src/routes/__tests__/learning-paths-schemas.test.ts) — 9 cases against `AttachCourseSchema`. The relevant route schemas were promoted from `const` to `export const` so the tests can import them. **Follow-ups (separate gaps, not blocking this one):** add an ESLint rule banning `z.string().uuid()` outside `shared/validation.ts` so the next reviewer can't reintroduce the bug; backfill schema tests for the remaining course-authoring + admin-articles + scorecards routes (only the directly-affected `ProgressUpdate` flow has full coverage today). |
